L1168: ## 10.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1169: ## 10.5.1. Venous Thromboembolism (VTE)
L1170: VTE is an AE of significant interest with CHC use. There were three VTEs in Study 300A and 1 VTE in Study 300B which are presented here. The VTEs occurred during cycles 2, 3, 6, and 7 of CVS use. All four subjects were past users of CHCs.
L1171: While Study 300A was ongoing, the Data Safety Monitoring Board (DSMB) met (August 22, 2007). At that time, two of the three VTEs (PE, DVT) had occurred and were reviewed. The meeting minutes are summarized below33:
L1172: At the DSMB meeting two serious adverse events (SAEs), one pulmonary embolism (PE) in one subject and one deep venous thrombosis (DVT) in one subject; both with a BMI 29.3 kg/m2 were reviewed. These two SAEs represented a larger rate of thrombosis than expected and were considered “not likely to be attributable to chance”. The DSMB felt possible explanations of the thrombotic events were:
L1173: - increased risk of thrombosis with increased BMI
- higher than expected serum EE levels with the study ring during initial cycles
L1174: (b) (4)
L1175: The DSMB noted that the EE levels for the study CVS are higher than NuvaRing EE levels in the initial and third cycle, although less than daily serum levels for the OrthoEvra patch by cycle 4.
L1176: The DSMB recommended against further enrollment of new subjects with a BMI &gt;29 kg/m2. For subjects of BMI &gt;29 kg/m2 and less than 35 y/o already in the study, the DSMB noted the greatest risk for elevated estrogen levels had passed and it was acceptable for these subjects to continue but that the study consents should be
L1177: reviewed to confirm there was adequate disclosure of possibly increased thrombosis risk.
L1178: After the recommendations, the applicant in agreement with DSMB decided to withdraw all subjects currently enrolled whose BMI was &gt;29 \text{ kg/m}^2 22.
L1179: In the context of VTE and BMI, the applicant [REDACTED] (b) (4)
L1180: **Study 300A Subject # [REDACTED] (b) (6) DVT, right calf**
A 26-year-old nonsmoker with a screening BMI of 30.8 who had previously used COCs and a vaginal contraceptive ring, started the CVS [REDACTED] (b) (6). Concomitant medications included Valtrex, taken [REDACTED] (b) (6) through [REDACTED] (b) (6). She experienced right leg pain and swelling on [REDACTED] (b) (6) (Cycle 6 Day 3). On [REDACTED] (b) (6), the subject went to the ER and had a lower extremity venous duplex performed showing acute deep vein thrombosis of the right popliteal and posterior tibial veins. Vital signs were normal. The subject was started on enoxaparin and warfarin, and was sent home with instructions to discontinue the CVS. The last day she used the ring was [REDACTED] (b) (6). Follow-up laboratory testing after anticoagulant therapy was stopped was negative for lupus anticoagulant, Factor V Leiden and Factor II DNA mutation and showed normal activity in Antithrombin III, Protein C Activity, Activated Protein C Resistance, Protein S-Functional, Factor II activity and D-Dimer. This subject had a confounding factor of driving for five hours one week prior to the VTE event. The Investigator classified this AE as probably related to CVS use. The DVT resolved with treatment.
L1181: **Comment:** Subject [REDACTED] (b) (6) experienced a DVT while exposed to three risk factors for VTE. COC use increases risk of VTE 2-4 times compared to nonusers, obesity (&gt;30 \text{ kg/m}^2) further increases VTE risk in COC users 2.3-4.6 fold over non-obese COC users, and travel increases the relative risk of VTE by 2.8 compared to non-travelers23. We agree that all of these factors may have contributed to the VTE, however, we believe the emphasis on this subject's BMI is overstated. First, obesity is defined by &gt;30 \text{ kg/m}^2 and the use of BMI &gt; 29 \text{ kg/m}^2 is arbitrary and determined based on the VTEs experienced in the clinical study. Second, review of clinic data shows the subject's weight decreased during her use of the CVS. The day after her DVT was diagnosed [REDACTED] (b) (6), she was seen in the clinic and her BMI was 29.3 \text{ kg/m}^2. At her previous visit in [REDACTED] (b) (6), her BMI was 30.1 \text{ kg/m}^2. Clearly, subject [REDACTED] (b) (6) hovered at the overweight/obese cut off and the five pound difference between her April visit and July VTE event doesn't, physiologically speaking, seem a

L1185: ## **Study 300A Subject #** (b) (6) Pulmonary Embolism
L1186: A 23-year-old with a screening BMI of 29.1, who had previously used COCs and transdermal contraception, started the CVS (b) (6). On (b) (6), the subject experienced numbness and tingling in the toes of both feet for a short time, but denied leg pain. On (b) (6) (Cycle 2) the subject experienced back and chest pain with difficulty breathing. On (b) (6), she was admitted to the hospital and computed tomography and pulmonary angiogram showed filling defects in the lingular segmental pulmonary artery, a subsegment of the posterior basal pulmonary artery in the right lower lobe, and the apical segmental pulmonary artery to the right upper lobe just past the bifurcation of the truncus anterior, giving a diagnosis of PE. Laboratory test results on admission were within normal range; tests for thrombophilia and for hemostatic factors were negative. She was treated with anticoagulants and discharged from the hospital on (b) (6). Her last CVS use was (b) (6). The applicant noted her screening BMI was 29.1. Her BMI at hospitalization was 30.2. The Investigator classified this AE as definite/highly probable in relation to CVS use. The PE resolved with treatment.
L1187: **Comment:** Subject (b) (6) experienced an unprovoked PE with a negative hypercoagulability workup during her second cycle of CVS use. The applicant notes her only risk factor was her BMI of &gt; 29 \text{ kg/m}^2. We do believe that the patient's BMI alone precipitated her PE since her BMI was &gt; 29 \text{ kg/m}^2 at baseline and she had no prior history of DVT/PE despite her previous exposure of COCs. Review of BMI measurements shows the following:
L1188: Visit	Date	BMI	Day of CVS Use
VO	(b) (6)	29.1	screening
V1	28.5	baseline day 1
Hospital/PE	30.2	day 42
V5	28.0	day 47
V6	27.8	day 54
L1189: The applicant suggests that a BMI over 29 \text{ kg/m}^2 incurs a tenfold higher risk for VTE. Indeed, PE risk goes up as BMI increases beyond 22.5 \text{ kg/m}^2 and the risk increases almost six fold at \ge 35 \text{ kg/m}^2 compared to those with \text{BMI} &lt; 22.5 \text{ kg/m}^2 (Kabrhel et al 2012).24 From the subject's BMI measurements, her BMI was &gt; 28 \text{ kg/m}^2 before initiation of CVS and her BMI change on CVS was not remarkable; therefore, a temporal association between exposure to CVS and the occurrence of PE cannot be excluded.
L1190: 24 Kabrhel C, Varraso R, Goldhaber S, Rimm EB, Camargo CA. Prospective study of BMI and risk of pulmonary embolism in women. *Obesity*. 2009;17:2040-2046
L1191: ## **Study 300A Subject #** (b) (6) DVT, Factor V heterozygous mutation
L1192: A 39-year-old with a baseline BMI of 24.7, history of hypothyroidism treated with Synthroid, and previous use of COC for 6 years (last use (b) (6) ago), and one pregnancy 15 years prior, started the CVS (b) (6). On (b) (6) (Cycle 3) she experienced left leg pain and swelling. On (b) (6) an “ultrasound scan” of leg was negative. The subject had increasing pain and difficulty walking and a second “ultrasound scan” was performed on (b) (6) with the diagnosis of acute DVT in left lower extremity involving the popliteal, peroneal and gastrocnemius veins at which time she discontinued the CVS. The subject was started on enoxaparin and warfarin and the CVS was discontinued. This subject was in the sub-study for hepatic factors and at baseline visit, (b) (6), her labs were normal. (b) (6) laboratory tests showed that she was positive for one copy Factor V mutation (heterozygous); the rest of thrombophilia labs were normal or negative. The Investigator classified this AE as possibly related to CVS. The DVT resolved with treatment.
L1193: **Comment:** Subject (b) (6) experienced a DVT and was found to have a Factor V Leiden mutation on subsequent thrombophilia laboratory testing. Presence of this mutation in women using OCs increases the risk of VTE 4-8 fold over women using OCs without the mutation. Screening for Factor V Leiden mutation prior to OC use is not recommended due to the prevalence (5% of women) and the low incidence of venous thrombosis-related mortality33.
L1194: ## **Study 300B Subject** (b) (6) Cerebral Venous Thrombosis
L1195: 28-year-old former smoker (quit in (b) (6) with a history of depression and use of COC five weeks prior to study entry, started the CVS (b) (6) with a BMI of 25.0 kg/m2. On (b) (6) (Cycle 7 day 24) during the ring-out period, the subject experienced a localized headache that progressed during the night and was accompanied with nausea and vomiting. The next day the pain returned and worsened. On (b) (6), a magnetic resonance angiography of the head “showed near total occlusive thrombosis of the left transverse sinus and no evidence of intracranial arterial aneurysm, occlusion or flow limiting stenosis”. The subject was admitted to the hospital and treated with heparin. Laboratory tests on admission were within normal ranges. The subject was discharged from the hospital one week later on coumadin. Attempts to contact the subject by the study site were unsuccessful. The subject’s physician verbally stated that the subject’s coagulation profile laboratory tests were all normal. The Investigator classified this AE as possibly related to CVS use.
L1196: **Comment:** Subject (b) (6) experienced an apparent unprovoked cerebral venous thrombosis. The applicant was not able to obtain hospital records or follow up with the subject because the subject did not return calls nor had she given permission for medical records release. Her primary care physician verbally stated that “the subject was doing well and that her coagulation profile laboratory tests were all normal” to the Principal Investigator.

L1205: **Comment:** We do not agree with the applicant's proposed
L1206: (b) (4)
L1207: 25 Report of DSMB Meeting Minutes for Protocol CCN006 (300A &amp; 300B) 22 Aug 2007. Submitted NDA 209627 Modules 1.6.3 Correspondence Regarding Meetings.
L1208: (b) (4)
L1209: Based on clinical review, the CVS VTE risk should be evaluated including all subjects and cycles in the primary ISS, [REDACTED] (b) (4) Based on the primary ISS of 2308 women contributing 21,590 cycles with 4 VTEs, the cumulative incidence rate is 24.1 per 10,000 women-years (95%CI 6.6, 61.7). Although this is a somewhat higher incidence than noted in other recent CHC trials, the increased rate is due to one additional VTE event occurring in the trials. Further, these clinical studies were not powered to provide a precise estimate of the incidence of uncommon events such as VTEs, which is reflected in the wide confidence interval that speaks to the considerable uncertainty associated with the VTE incidence rate estimate of 24.1 per 10,000 women-years estimate.
L1210: There have been numerous studies on VTE risk in CHCs, especially in relation to type of progestin. FDA labels show the likelihood of developing a VTE for a CHC-user of 3-12/10,000 women-years. The American College of Obstetrician and Gynecologists Committee Opinion 540 (reaffirmed 2016) provides the risk of VTE among COC users as 3-9/10,000 women-years26 while the EURAS Study27 has 10.2/10,000 women-years for “other OCs” (other than LNG COCs and Yasmin).
L1211: In the past 10 years, approved CHCs with the highest VTE incidence per 10,000 women-years based on clinical trial data are 16.7 for Lo Loestrin Fe (EE/NETA) and 11.3 for Quartette (EE/LNG). The absolute numbers of VTEs were small with Quartette having 3 VTEs and the rest of the CHCs having 0-2 VTEs. NuvaRing clinical trials which excluded women with a BMI &gt;29 kg/m2 was approved in 2001 and had a VTE incidence of 5.3 per 10,000 women-years. The OrthoEvra patch clinical trials had 2 VTEs for a 11.7 VTE/10,000 women-years (approved 2001).
L1212: The applicant notes the mean BMI of 26.7 kg/m2 for the CVS is higher than other CHCs, and this may have increased the number of VTEs in the pivotal trials. However, Quartette trial subjects had a mean BMI of 27.3 kg/m2 and LoSeasonique trial subjects had a mean BMI of 26.8 kg/m2. LoSeasonique had no VTEs in the clinical trials. Therefore, BMI is not the reason the VTE estimate appears high in this submission.
L1213: 26 https://www.acog.org/Clinical-Guidance-and-Publications/Committee-Opinions/Committee-on-Gynecologic-Practice/Risk-of-Venous-Thromboembolism
L1214: 27 Dinger J, Heinemann L, Kühl-Habich D. 2007. The Safety of a drospirenone-containing oral contraceptive: final results from the European Active Surveillance Study on oral contraceptives based on 142,475 women-years of observation. Contraception; 75(5): 344-354.
L1215: The applicant states that three of the four subjects with VTE had predisposing factors (driving for an extended time, positive Factor V, and BMI). While this is true, subject recruitment in this contraceptive study was consistent with other contraceptive studies. As in other contraceptive studies, in the studies for this application, subjects with known risks for VTE (i.e. family history or personal history of VTE, hypertension, migraines with focal symptoms, smokers over 35 years old) were excluded. These studies are designed to reflect how the contraceptive product will be used in practice, with the above exclusions listed as contraindications in the label. Since many women are not aware of their personal risk for VTE both in clinical studies and in clinical practice, this study design reflects real-world evidence. Of note, all 4 subjects had previously used CHCs and would be classified as "previous users" (4 weeks had elapsed since last use) and might be considered at lower risk than naïve subjects.
L1216: **Comment:** After discussions with the applicant, the identified VTE risk will be labeled in Warnings and Precautions (W&amp;P) as in all CHCs. Within the W &amp; P section, obesity as a risk factor for VTE is listed. I recommend discussion of the four (4) VTEs in the Clinical Trials experience. Whether to include subject level data, i.e. BMI and other risk factors, will be determined. Because we cannot draw a definitive conclusion about the actual VTE risk for the CVS based on the available data or how it would compare to other CHC products, we will require a postmarketing study to evaluate VTE risk with an appropriately powered required study to gain a more accurate risk assessment.
L1217: ## 10.5.2. Angioedema/Drug Allergy
L1218: Hereditary angioedema is a rare familial disorder that is associated with exogenous estrogens (i.e. CHCs) or high estrogen levels (as in pregnancy). Drug allergies with CHCs are more common and have diverse etiologies. Two SAEs in the Angioedema/Drug Allergy category occurred in the pivotal trials.
L1219: **Study 300A Subject #** (b) (6) Combination Drug Allergy, Angioedema
A 37-year-old who in the previous year used NuvaRing, used the CVS (b) (6) through (b) (6) and on (b) (6) experience angioedema, with throat constriction, respiratory wheezing and hives. She self-treated with Benadryl but later went to the emergency room and was treated with solumedrol and prednisone. She returned to the emergency room for the next 3 days (b) (6), was treated symptomatically and never admitted. The subject had been suffering from a viral illness and was using aspirin products. She had an evaluation by an allergist (b) (6) and the allergist made the diagnosis of angioedema/urticaria. The allergist felt the viral illness and use of NSAIDS and opioid therapy were contributing factors but that the reaction can be related to hormone therapy. The subject discontinued the CVS and restarted NuvaRing on (b) (6) with complete recovery from the event on (b) (6). The investigator classified this AE as probably related.
L1220: **Comment:** Angioedema is an uncommon but known event that occurs with the estrogen
L1221: *component of CHCs. This subject's symptoms of angioedema occurred within one month of starting the CVS and continued despite daily treatment until the CVS was discontinued. The episode resolved after the CVS was discontinued but while the subject was using NuvaRing. This is an interesting case because the symptoms of angioedema resolved despite using Nuvaring which contains the same estrogen component; therefore, it is possible the subject's angioedema is due to other components of CVS that is not ethinyl estradiol.*
L1222: ## **Study 300B Subject # [REDACTED](b) (6) Urticaria, Facial Edema, Asthmatic Attack**
L1223: A 33-year-old with a history of asthma and several allergies including nickel and penicillin and past use of CHCs, started the CVS [REDACTED](b) (6). On [REDACTED](b) (6), she “got a cold” and increased her asthma medication. Two day later she experienced urticaria mainly on her arms one hour after eating cashews. She had no history of allergy to cashew nuts. She was treated with cortisone and antihistaminic medication with the rash decreasing on [REDACTED](b) (6). However, on [REDACTED](b) (6) her urticaria increased and she experience facial edema. She removed the CVS on [REDACTED](b) (6). With continued treatment for asthma and allergic reaction, she recovered. There were no genital symptoms suggesting a local allergic reaction. The applicant felt the nuts were more likely the causal factor and that a slow recovery from the allergic reaction most likely explained the clinical resolution rather than allergic reaction to the CVS with a positive dechallenge test.
L1224: **Comment:** *Hypersensitivity to the CVS or any of its components will be listed in the Contraindications section of the label. Angioedema will be labeled in the Warnings section.*
L1225: ## 10.6. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L1226: There were no patient reported safety outcomes that required input from the clinical outcome assessment team regarding safety or tolerability.
L1227: ## 10.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1228: The applicant presented MedDRA-based tables for their demographic subgroups of age, race, ethnicity and geographic region. There were no significant differences in the proportion or the types of TEAEs in any of the demographic subgroups.
L1229: Age was categorized by &lt;36 years or ≥36 years. There were 1872 TEAEs in the &lt;36 year group and 144 TEAEs in the ≥36 years group. Percentages for the different SOC and PT were similar between these groups.
L1230: Race was categorized by Caucasian, Black, and Other. There were 1429 TEAEs in the Caucasian, 261 TEAEs in the Black, and 287 TEAEs in the Other groups. Percentages for the different SOC and PT were similar for the three race categories.
L1231: The TEAEs between Hispanic and non-Hispanic, nulliparity and parity, US and non-US, and age of the CVS (&lt;1.69 years, &gt;1.69-1.99 years, ≥ 2 years) subgroups were similar.
L1232: ## 10.8. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L1233: Nested within Study 300A, were three sub-studies to explore the effect of the CVS on vaginal microbiology, endometrial histology, and four estrogen-dependent hepatic proteins. The sub-studies procedures and results discussed in this section (10.8 Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials) relate to the specific safety parameters.
L1234: ## 10.8.1. Microbiology sub-study
L1235: The primary objective of the Microbiology sub-study was to evaluate changes in vaginal flora and risk of vaginal infection associated with use of the same CVS for 13 cycles.
L1236: The trial design was a single center sub-study of 300A where the subjects were also full participants in study 300A and included in the contraceptive efficacy and safety assessments in the main study. A separate and additional informed consent was obtained at enrollment into the microbiology sub-study.
L1237: In addition to the visits and procedures required for 300A, subjects in this sub-study had quantitative vaginal cultures, vaginal smear saline preparation, potassium hydroxide analysis, vaginal pH, Whiff test, gram stain, and temperature assessments at Screening (V0), cycle 6 (V3), and cycle 13 (V5). At Visit 5 (cycle 13) when the study ring was removed, quantitative culture of the vaginal ring was also performed. If subjects did not complete 13 cycles, they were to complete all of the cycle 13 assessments at their final study visit, regardless of when the final visit occurred. Visit 3 occurred on day 1-7 (in week 1 of CVS use) and Visit 5 occurred on day 21-27 (during last week of the last treatment cycle).
L1238: The endpoints for this sub-study were:
L1239: - the presence of bacterial vaginosis based on Amsels criteria and Nugent score
L1240: ## Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA209627
## ANNOVERA (segesterone acetate and ethinyl estradiol vaginal system)
L1241: - presence of yeast based on KOH analysis
- presence and amount of WBCs
- presence or absence of trichomonads
- vaginal cultures presence of Lactobacillus, Gardnerella vaginalis, enterococcus, Staphylococcus aureus, E. coli, Candida albicans, other yeast, anaerobic gram-negative
L1242: Comparison between baseline (VO) and follow up visit (V6 and/or V13) was performed. Amsels and Nugent score were determined by the principal investigator. The criteria are presented in the Appendix (22.6.2 Microbiology Sub-study).
L1243: One hundred twenty (120) subjects were enrolled in the microbiology sub-study. One hundred and ten (110) subjects had a baseline and at least one follow-up visit and comprised the microbiology population. The disposition of the 120 subjects is shown in the table below.

L1256: **Comment:** The mean concentration of anaerobic gram negative rods (AGNR) increased with CVS use with the median concentrations remaining at a low level (\le 10^2) and no subjects with symptomatic infection suggesting that this increase is not clinically significant. The concentration of hydrogen peroxidase lactobacillus in vaginal flora cultures maintained high levels at all three assessments with no statistically significant changes based on shift tables. There was a decrease of non-hydrogen peroxidase lactobacillus between baseline and Cycle 13 that was statistically significant; this finding is not likely to be clinically significant since hydrogen peroxide-producing lactobacillus helps provide a normal vaginal ecosystem. The KOH results reported by the applicant separate the KOH results into symptomatic and asymptomatic, which I have combined in the above table. It is difficult to ascribe clinical significance to such small numbers; 2 vs 3 subjects.
L1257: Of concern is the concomitant use of fluconazole by 19 subjects during this study. It is unclear how this affects the results, especially for yeast. Also, the subjects of this sub-study, based on demographics, do not reflect the overall US population and it is
L1258: *unknown if these results are generalizable to the US or global populations. In summary, none of the data obtained from this substudy shows a new trend or concern that would require specific labeling for vaginal infections.*
L1259: ## 10.8.2. Endometrial Safety Sub-Study
L1260: The primary objective of the Endometrial safety sub-study was to demonstrate the safety of the CVS on the endometrium after 1 year of use.
L1261: The trial design was a multicenter (6 US centers) sub-study of 300A where the subjects were also full participants in study 300A and included in the contraceptive efficacy and safety assessments in the main study. A modified informed consent form with risks and benefits of the endometrial biopsies was signed prior to subjects' entry into the sub-study.
L1262: In addition to the visits and procedures required for 300A, subjects in this sub-study had two endometrial biopsies. All subjects in this sub-study (after a negative UPT) had an endometrial biopsy at Screening (V0). If the subject was not on CHCs, the biopsy was performed between days 7-14 of the subject's cycle. If the screening biopsy showed hyperplasia or carcinoma, the subject was to be excluded from enrolling in both this sub-study and the main study (300A). After CVS treatment started, the first 25 subjects to reach Cycle 6 (V3) were to have a second endometrial biopsy on Days 20-24 of Cycle 6. The remainder of the subjects were to have a second biopsy at Cycle 13 (V5) during Days 20-24 or at the time of early termination (provided the subject had been enrolled for at least 3 months).
L1263: Three pathologists with expertise in gynecology pathology read each of the processed endometrial tissue in a blinded fashion. The most severe diagnosis was recorded if there were differing diagnoses.
L1264: Endpoints included:
L1265: - Endometrial histology data summarized by visit and hormonal contraception history
- Changes in endometrial patterns from baseline
L1266: A total of 156 unique subjects had baseline endometrial biopsies and 83 subjects had a follow-up endometrial biopsy. Twenty seven (27) subjects had biopsies at Cycles 5 to 6, 25 subjects had biopsies at Cycles 7 to 11, and 31 subjects had biopsies at Cycles 12 to 13 and one subject had a biopsy performed 2 cycles after she finished using the CVS for 14 cycles. The disposition of the 156 subjects is shown in Table 62.

L1291: ## 10.8.3. Hepatic Factors Sub-Study
L1292: The primary objective of the hepatic factors sub-study was to evaluate the use of the CVS over 13 cycles on the effect of four (4) estrogen-dependent hepatic proteins: factor VIII, fibrinogen, Protein S, and sex hormone binding globulin (SHBG). Factor VIII, fibrinogen, and Protein S were selected due to their involvement in the coagulation system. The applicant notes that these
L1293: ## Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA209627
## ANNOVERA (segesterone acetate and ethinyl estradiol vaginal system)
L1294: proteins may be associated with risk of thromboembolism although there is no biological marker established as an acceptable predictor of VTE risk.
L1295: The trial design was a two-center (both US) sub-study of 300A where the subjects were also full participants in study 300A and included in the contraceptive efficacy and safety assessments in the main study.
L1296: In addition to the visits and procedures required for 300A, subjects in this sub-study had an additional blood drawn of 10 cc for the hepatic markers at Screening (V0), Cycle 6 (V3), and Cycle 13 (V5). If subjects did not complete 13 cycles, at their final visit all of the Cycle 13 assessments were obtained. Visit 3 occurred on day 1-7 (in week 1 of CVS use) and Visit 5 occurred on day 21-27 (during last week of the last treatment cycle).
L1297: The endpoints for this sub-study were:
L1298: - Changes in each hepatic protein over time
- Compare values between the group of women with previous CHC use and the group of women without previous CHC use
L1299: One hundred twenty-nine (129) subjects were enrolled in the hepatic factors sub-study. One hundred and six (106) subjects had a baseline value, 104 subjects had a Cycle 6 value, and 61 subjects at Cycle 13 (or final visit) value. The safety population included all subjects enrolled who inserted the CVS. The hepatic-evaluable population included all treated subjects with at least one follow-up laboratory results.

L1320: ## 10.8.4. Colposcopy
L1321: Study 180, a 13-cycle, multicenter, randomized, dose finding and efficacy study performed vaginal/cervical colposcopy on subjects at 3 of the 5 sites. The colposcopic examinations were performed at pretreatment, cycle 3, and cycle 13. Evaluation of the vagina and cervix was for epithelial abnormalities (e.g. abrasion, ectropion, ulcer, acetowhite, punctuation, vascularity). No severe abnormality was found at any time. The only finding that occurred in &gt;2% of the subjects on treatment was vaginal erythema in 6 subjects (4%) at Cycle 3 and cervical erythema in 5 subjects (3.3%). Findings suggestive of cervical intraepithelial abnormality were categorized as AEs and reported in the All SA/EE ISS.
L1322: **Comment:** There does not appear to be any safety signal with the CVS use in regards to cervical and vaginal epithelial integrity.
L1323: ## 10.8.5. Lactation Studies
L1324: There are no lactation studies conducted with the CVS. However, three lactation studies with SA implants have been performed and are summarized here.
L1325: An early study28 on 6 healthy lactating women using an implant with 20 mg SA evaluated the relationship between serum and breast milk SA levels. The average plasma concentration was 62 pg/ml and the average breast milk concentration was 38 pg/ml. The mean milk/plasma ratio was 0.6 with a range from 0.25 to 0.91.
L1326: Coutinho14 studied lactating women with SA implants (50 mg) and their nursing infants for one year. On Day 75 mean maternal serum levels of SA were 381 pmol/L, mean maternal breast milk levels of SA were 373 pmol/L, and the mean infant serum levels of SA were 19 pmol/L.
L1327: 28 Lahteenmaki PLA, Diaz S, Miranda P, et al. Milk and plasma concentrations of the progestin ST-1435 in women treated parenterally with ST-1435. Contraception 1990;42:555-562
L1328: There were no safety signals in feeding, growth and development of the infants observed between the SA and control groups (who used a Copper T380 IUD)29.
L1329: Massai30 in a controlled study using SA implants delivering 100 µg/day studied lactation and infant growth. Mean SA levels in maternal serum were 175 pmol/L in the first months decreasing to 60 pmol/L at the end of the first year. The mean SA levels in the breast milk were 135pmol/L decreasing to 54pmol/L. It was estimated that the infants received 50 ng/day of SA through breast feeding. The body weight of infants was similar between the control group (copper IUD) and the SA group over the 1 year study period.
L1330: **Comment:** Although these studies were with SA-only implants and contained lower doses of SA than the CVS, the results of SA in lactating women, breast milk, and nursing infants showed no safety signals in feeding, growth, and development between the segesterone acetate implant group and the control group. This information is included in labeling in addition to the general recommendation against breast feeding with use of contraceptives containing EE.

L1341: ## 10.9.4. Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L1342: No cases of toxicity due to overdose have been reported. The CVS is not believed to have any abuse or dependent potential. In addition, there is no evidence of impairment of fertility that would indicate a withdrawal-type phenomenon (See Section 10.4.12).
L1343: ## 10.10. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1344: (b) (4)
L1345: Based on this information, there is no data from postmarketing that will add to the safety profile from the applicant's data.
L1346: Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience Based on our review: Although the estimate for the VTE incidence rate has considerable uncertainty, it is possible that there may be a different or increased risk of thromboembolic disease with use of this CVS compared with other recently approved CHCs. However, the VTE risk should be further minimized with appropriate labeling, which can help prescribers select patients based on their low risk for VTE. The required postmarketing study will be necessary to determine whether the VTE risk with use of the CVS offers a different safety profile from that of other CHCs. At this time, labeling will reflect that the risk is likely comparable to that of other approved CHCs since the 95% CI of the CVS VTE incidence rate (6.6, 61.7) has some overlap with the 3-12 per 10,000 woman-years likelihood of developing a VTE for other CHC users.
L1347: 31 NDA 290627, Sequence 0018, submitted 4.26.2018, Response to IR, Module 1.11.4 p 10/11
L1348: 32 Email communication from Cherice Holloway CDER/OSE/OCT through Miriam Chehab
OMPT/CDER/OSE/OPE/DPVII May 4, 2018
L1349: ## **10.10.1. Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting** Based on the safety profile from subjects in the CVS trials, it appears that safety is expected to be similar to other approved CHC products. See Section 16.
L1350: ## **10.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety**
L1351: The safety assessment based on all submitted data (3,052 subjects with 26,794 cycles) for subjects treated with the TBM CVS and other formulations of SA and SA/EE CVSS indicates that the CVS has an acceptable safety profile. There were no new safety signals noted with the CVS which contains SA, an NME progestin. A post-marketing requirement (PMR) study to better characterize the VTE risk with use of the CVS is recommended because the submitted clinical trials are not powered for evaluation of VTE risk.
L1352: The safety findings in the TBM CVS population (primary ISS) with 2308 subjects contributing 21,590 cycles are summarized.
L1353: Subjects in the ISS were mostly Caucasian (71%), non-Hispanic (70%), nulliparous (64%), from the US (67%) with a mean age of 26.7 years and a mean BMI of 24.1 kg/m2
L1354: No deaths occurred.
L1355: Forty-three subjects or 1.9% of the primary ISS experienced an SAE.
L1356: There were four VTEs. Two DVTs occurred in two subjects; one who had driven for 5 hours a week prior to diagnosis and the other in a subject subsequently found to be heterozygous for Factor V Leiden mutation. There was one PE in a subject with normal laboratory evaluation. Another subject experienced a cerebral venous thrombosis and had unconfirmed normal laboratory testing. All four subjects had histories of previous CHC use.
L1357: The four VTEs in the pivotal studies give a higher incidence of VTE than other contraceptive pivotal studies. However, this higher incidence is based on one additional VTE. Since the clinical trials were not powered to evaluate a relatively uncommon event (VTE), the risk estimate is imprecise. A PMR will be performed to provide a more accurate risk assessment.
L1358: VTE is a known risk of CHC use. Risk based on differences in COC formulations show VTE rates of 7.2-9.8 VTE/10,000 women-years with no statistically significant difference between types of progestin or days of use. The class labeling for VTE risk will be included in the CVS label. (b) (4)
L1359: (b) (4)
L1360: The applicant's data on BMI and VTE risk
L1361: Two VTEs occurred in subjects with a BMI 28-31 kg/m2 and two VTEs occurred in subjects with a BMI 24-26 kg/m2. Only one subject had a BMI &gt;29 kg/m2 at the time of VTE and
L1362: ## Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA209627
## ANNOVERA (segesterone acetate and ethinyl estradiol vaginal system)
L1363: this BMI was 29.3 kg/m2. Physiologically speaking this small difference in BMI should not increase VTE risk by tenfold.
L1364: There were four psychiatric events in four subjects with psychiatric history. There was one attempted suicide in a subject with depression. The remaining three events involved exacerbation of bipolar disorder. From a clinical perspective, the information in these events did not generate a new safety signal or trend for this CVS that required labeling that deviated from class labeling for CHCs.
L1365: Other SAEs included two subjects with drug hypersensitivity reactions (neither involved anaphylaxis), one subject with menorrhagia due to a worsening submucous fibroid, and one subject with headache, nausea, vomiting, and diarrhea.
L1366: Adverse events leading to discontinuation occurred in 275 subjects (11.9%) of the ISS. The most frequent AEs were vaginal symptoms (including discharge and infection) - 2.1%, menstrual disorders - 1.7%, mood changes - 1.4%, headache with migraine - 1.3%, nausea and vomiting - 1.2%. CVS expulsion was not considered an AE but was the reason 1.4% of subjects discontinued.
L1367: Common AEs experienced by CVS users did not show any additional safety issues that are noted in other CHCs although the incidences of many AEs were higher in CVS users. The frequency of the AEs are summarized in the table below.

L1373: **Comment:** AEs experienced by ≥5% of subjects will be labeled.
L1374: At least one complete CVS expulsion was reported by 25% of subjects, however CVS expulsion was the reason for discontinuation of CVS use in only 1.4% of subjects..
L1375: Laboratory findings did not suggest any safety signals. There was no significant reduction or improvement of hemoglobin/hematocrit. There were elevations of cholesterol, HDL, LDL, triglycerides, and liver enzymes that were consistent with CHCs.
L1376: Physical examinations including vital signs did not show safety signals although findings of vaginal discharge and vaginal and vulvar erythema were noted.
L1377: There were no issues concerning pregnancy outcomes or return to fertility.
L1378: There were no differences in the safety profile of the CVS, based on TEAEs, within demographic subgroups of age, race, ethnicity, and geographic region. Safety profiles were similar between the ages of the CVS subgroups.
L1379: Specific safety studies were reviewed and did not show any new or unique concerns outside of those previously identified for CHCs.
L1380: - QT studies showed no significant QTc prolongation effect of SA
- Microbiology studies showed no increases in symptomatic bacterial vaginosis, yeast, or anaerobic gram-negative rods
- Endometrial biopsies did not find evidence of hyperplasia or malignancy
- Estrogen-dependent hepatic proteins showed the same trends as found in other CHCs
- Colposcopic evaluations did not find evidence of vaginal or cervical epithelial integrity abnormalities other than erythema in &lt;5% of subjects
L1381: **Comment:** The safety data was sufficient to conclude that the profile for this product does not appear to be significantly different from other CHCs except for the potential for a difference in VTE risk. As previously stated, in order to better determine if this VTE risk is different from other CHC products, a large postmarketing required study will be
L1382: *necessary. The applicant understands this concern and has agreed to conduct this study using acceptable required milestones.*

L1404: ## 16. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L1405: It is expected that the CVS will be used chronically and that users will have a risk of thromboembolic disease. The size of the clinical trial for the CVS did not allow determination of whether this risk was different from the risks seen with other CHCs. The review team and the Division of Epidemiology agreed that this risk needed to be further characterized through a large epidemiologic study and communicated this to the applicant. The details of this study are outlined below:
L1406: - A controlled, non-interventional, long-term cohort study that follows a series of cohorts comprising new users of your product (segesterone acetate and ethinyl estradiol vaginal system), new users of other ring contraceptives, new users of any intrauterine system, and new users of combined oral contraceptives containing other progestins. The primary objective of the study is to assess the risk for venous thromboembolism (VTE) and arterial thromboembolism (ATE) of short-term and long-term use of your product in a study population representative of actual users of the product in the United States and other countries where your vaginal system is prescribed.The study should be sufficiently powered to rule out a 1.5 to 2-fold risk for VTE
L1407: (b) (4)
L1408: Two drug interaction studies were recommended by the Clinical Pharmacology and Clinical reviewers. These required studies are outlined below:
L1409: - A clinical drug-drug interaction study to evaluate the effects of strong CYP3A induction and inhibition on the pharmacokinetics of SA and EE from your SA/EE contraceptive vaginal system (CVS) (See Section 6.3.1 for the rationale of this study request).
- An [REDACTED] (b) (4) study to evaluate the effects of tampons on the [REDACTED] (b) (4) of SA and EE from your SA/EE CVS.
L1410: ## Postmarketing Commitments
L1411: The CMC review team determined that the Applicant had not fully characterized the in vivo release rate of the CVS based on residual drug collected at one time point at the end of 13 cycles. Therefore, to accurately determine the strength of the product for labeling, the “true” in vivo release rate should be calculated using data collected throughout the period of use. Under a PMC, [REDACTED] (b) (4)

L1435: ## 17.5.2. Microbiology Sub-study
L1436: - The diagnosis of bacterial vaginosis was determined by the Investigator utilizing the Amsel criteria. The presence of three of the following four criteria gave the diagnosis: a homogenous excessive vaginal discharge
-  an alkaline vaginal pH (&gt; 4.5)
-  a positive whiff test
-  the presence of 20% or more clue cells
-  [bacterial vaginosis was also categorized as symptomatic or asymptomatic]
- The Investigator also determined the diagnosis of bacterial vaginosis utilizing the Nugent score. A score &gt; 7 indicates bacterial vaginosis. The scores were categorized as: Normal flora (Lactobacillus - predominant) - scores 0 to 3
-  Intermediate flora - scores 4 to 6
-  Bacterial vaginosis - scores 7 to 10
L1437: Source Microbiology Sub-study Clinical Study Report P6

L1455: ## Radioimmunoassay (RIA)
L1456: For earlier studies including Study 124, 174, 180 and 181, serum concentrations of SA were determined using a validated RIA technique developed at the University of Helsinki, Finland1. Briefly, this was a conventional RIA where serum samples were first extracted with petroleum ether. After processing, samples were incubated with an antibody to SA and a radiolabeled SA, free steroid was separated from bound steroid using dextran-charcoal, and the amount of radioactivity remaining in the supernatant fluids was determined. The LLOQ for SA was 13 pmol/L (4.8 pg/mL). Serum concentrations of EE were determined using RIA method developed by the USC Medical Center, Los Angeles, CA2. The LLOQ for EE was 10 pg/mL. The detailed method and validation parameters are described in Table 2.
L1457: 1 Lähteenmäki P, Weiner E, Lähteenmäki P, et al. Contraception with subcutaneous capsules containing ST-1435. Pituitary and ovarian function and plasma levels of ST-1435. Contraception. 1981; 23(1):63-75.
L1458: 2 Brenner PF, Goebelsmann U, Stanczyk FZ, et al. Serum levels of ethinylestradiol following its ingestion alone or in oral contraceptive formulations. Contraception. 1980; 22(1):85-95.

L1461: ## 2. Clinical BA/BE Assessments
L1462: ## 2.1. Study 300PK (Pivotal PK/PD Study)
L1463: **Title:** A multicenter, open-label study to evaluate the PK, PD, cycle control and safety of a contraceptive vaginal ring delivering a daily dose of 150 µg of SA and 15 µg of EE (150/15 SA/EE CVS)
L1464: ## **Objectives:**
L1465: - To evaluate the effects of a new SA/EE CVS on ovulation suppression
- To assess the PK of SA and EE during Cycles 1, 3 and 13 of CVS use.
L1466: **Study Design:** This was an open-label, non-randomized PK/ PD study of SA/EE CVS on a 21/7 day usage cycles in 39 healthy female subjects. Subjects were allowed to remove the CVS for short periods of time (up to 2 hours) for washing or sexual intercourse. The total duration of treatment is thirteen 28-day cycles (approximately 1 year).
L1467: Blood samples for PK analysis were collected before CVS insertion and after insertion on Day 1 (at 1, 2, 4 and 8 hours post-dose), Days 2, 3, 4, 5, 8, 15 and 22 of Cycles 1, 3, and 13. Additional blood samples were collected on Days 23, 24, 25, and 28/29 in Cycle 1 to determine the PK of SA and EE after CVS was removed. The primary PD assessment in this study was serum progesterone (P) concentration, a marker for suppression of ovulation. Estradiol (E2) concentrations were also measured to provide additional information on luteal activity. Ovulation was considered to have occurred when two consecutive P measurements, not including the first one done at pre-ring insertion, were \ge 10 nmol/L. Blood samples for PD analysis were collected on Days 1, 4, 8, 12, 15, 18, 22, 25 and 29 of Cycles 1, 3, and 13.
L1468: Serum concentrations of SA and EE were measured using a validated GC-MS/MS assay developed by (b) (4) Details of the assay can be found in Bioanalytical Report No. 3836.083107.
L1469: **PK Results:** The study subjects were healthy, mostly Hispanic women with a mean age of 29.1 (\pm 6.4 SD) years and a mean BMI of 26.18 (range: 16.89 - 37.46) \text{kg}/\text{m}^2. Thirty (30) out of 39 subjects completed the study. The serum concentration-time profiles of SA and EE are shown in Figure 1 with PK parameters summarized in Table 3. Following vaginal administration, SA and EE were absorbed into systemic circulation with median T_{\text{max}} around 2 hours in Cycles 1, 3, and 13. Concentrations of both components declined after T_{\text{max}} and became more constant after 96 hours postdose. Mean (SD) maximum SA concentration (C_{\text{max}}) in Cycle 1 was 1147 (315) pg/mL and decreased over subsequent cycles of use. Specifically, C_{\text{max}} of SA were 363 (133) pg/mL in Cycle 3 and 294 (116) pg/mL in Cycle 13. Similar trend was observed with EE. Mean (SD) C_{\text{max}} of EE was 129 (39) pg/mL in Cycle 1. The mean EE C_{\text{max}} in Cycles 3 and 13 were approximately one-half and one-third, respectively, of the mean value reported in Cycle 1. Observed C_{\text{max}} values in Cycle 1, Cycle 3 and Cycle 13 suggested that (b) (4) release of SA and EE from this CVS was most significant upon initial use and became less prominent in subsequent cycles.

L1481: **PD Results:** Figure 3 are plots of individual P and E2 levels observed in Cycle 1, Cycle 3 and Cycle 13. Three subjects were excluded from the analysis based on undetectable SA concentrations in the tested cycles (see note below). The mean (SD) P concentrations were 1.59 (1.88) nmol/L for Cycle 1, 1.24 (0.70) nmol/L for Cycle 3, and 1.60 nmol/L (3.64) for Cycle 13. No study subjects had luteal activity as defined as two consecutive P measurements of more than 10 nmol/L. One subject had serum P concentrations &lt; 10 nmol/L in Cycle 13. Serum E2 concentrations were also suppressed during the 21-day period of the cycle when the CVS was in place. Overall, these data suggest that when the SA/EE CVS was used as directed, serum progesterone concentrations remained adequately suppressed.
L1482: **Figure 3. P and E2 Serum Concentrations for Cycle 1 (N=39), Cycle 3 (N=35), and Cycle 13 (N=27)**
L1483: **Safety Results:** Thirty-nine (39) subjects were included for the safety analysis. The treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) reported by the highest percentages of subjects were nasopharyngitis (51%), headache (49%), and nausea (33%). The majority (67%) of subjects reported TEAEs considered by the Investigator to be mild or moderate. Twelve (31%) of 39 subjects reported TEAEs considered severe. The most commonly reported severe TEAEs were migraine (15%) and headache (13%). No clinically significant changes were noted for vital signs, ECGs, or clinical laboratory test results.
L1484: ## **Pregnancy:**
L1485: During this study, 1 subject (Subject (b)(6)) became pregnant in Cycle 10. This subject had no luteal activity on Cycle 1 and Cycle 3. Unfortunately, the pregnancy occurred during a cycle in which PK analysis of NES/EE serum concentrations were not conducted.
L1486: ## **Reviewer's Comments:**
L1487: - *SA/EE CVS in obese subjects: About 50% of study subjects were overweight (30 \text{ kg/m}^2 \ge \text{BMI} \ge 25 \text{ kg/m}^2) or obese (\text{BMI} \ge 30 \text{ kg/m}^2). As shown in Figure 4, increased body weight associated with decreased concentrations of SA and EE.*
L1488: **Figure 4. Box-plot Comparison of SA and EE Systemic Exposure Grouped by BMI (Study 300PK)**
L1489: - **Bioanalytical Analysis:** All study samples were analyzed within the established stability of 999 days for EE (maximum interval was 338 days). For SA, all samples were analyzed within the established stability of 405 days, except for one sample (463 days) from Subject (b) (6) in cycle 1, treatment day 1 before CVS insertion (Time 0). Considering the expected concentrations for EE and SA is zero for this timepoint, this reviewer considers the long-term stability data support the duration of sample storage.

L1503: ## 3. Clinical Drug Interaction Assessments
L1504: ## 3.1. Study 571 (DDI Study)
L1505: **Title:** An open-label study to evaluate the effects of concurrent administration of vaginal antimycotic medication (miconazole nitrate) on the PK of SA and EE delivered by a vaginal ring releasing a daily dose of 150 µg of SA and 15 µg of EE (150/15 SA /EE CVS) in normal ovulating women
L1506: ## **Objectives:**
L1507: - To evaluate the PK of EE and SA released from CVS in the presence of a single-dose and multiple-dose of antimycotic co-medication (miconazole nitrate suppository or cream)
- To evaluate the safety and tolerability of the CVS during two cycles of use in the presence or absence of antimycotic co-medication
L1508: **Study Design:** This was an open-label, randomized, crossover, DDI study in 45 healthy female subjects. This study had both single and three-day repeat dose cohorts (**Figure 9**). Subjects in the cohorts were enrolled sequentially, beginning with the single-dose suppository, followed by the multiple-dose suppository and ending with the multiple-dose cream. Subjects in Cohort 1 were administered 1200 mg miconazole nitrate delivered as a vaginal suppository once on Day 8 of the treatment cycle. Subjects in Cohort 2 were administered 200 mg miconazole nitrate delivered as a once daily suppository for three consecutive days. Subjects in Cohort 3 were administered 200 mg miconazole nitrate delivered as a once daily cream for three consecutive days. Cohorts 2 and 3 were administered miconazole nitrate on Days 8, 9, and 10 during their respective treatment periods. During the study, sex hormones, other forms of hormonal contraception, vaginal lubricants containing mineral oil, silicone based lubricants, tampons and and medications, such as isotretinoin, requiring the use of concomitant effective contraception, were prohibited. Water-based vaginal lubricants, such as K-Y Jelly and Replens, were permitted.
L1509: Serum concentrations of SA and EE were measured using a validated GC-MS/MS assay developed by [REDACTED] (b) (4) Details of the assay can be found in Bioanalytical Report No. 8493.101514.
L1510: ## Figure 9. Schematic Overview of the Study Design

L1513: - Cohort 1: A total of 19 blood samples (10 mL/sample) were collected in Cohort 1. On Day 8, serial blood samples were collected before and at 2, 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 36 and 48 hours after miconazole treatment. Single blood samples collected on days 1 (before CVS insertion), 2, 12, 14, 15, 16, 18, 20, 21 and 22 (day of CVS removal).
- Cohort 2 and Cohort 3: A total of 23 blood samples (10 mL/sample) were collected in Cohort 2 and Cohort 3. On Day 8 and Day 9, serial blood samples were collected before (0 hour) and at 8 and 16 hours after miconazole treatment. On Day 10, blood samples were collected before and at 2, 4, 8, 12, 16 and 24 hours after miconazole treatment. Single blood samples collected on days 1 (before CVS insertion), 2, 12, 14, 15, 16, 18, 20, 21 and 22 (day of CVS removal).
L1514: **PK Results:** Twenty nice (29) subjects completed the study. Demographics and baseline characteristics were comparable for all three cohorts.
L1515: ## Cohort 1: Effect of a Single-Dose of Miconazole Nitrate Suppository on the PK of SA and EE from CVS
L1516: PK profiles of SA and EE from CVS with and without a single-dose of vaginal miconazole suppository on Day 8 are shown in **Figure 10** with PK parameters summarized in **Table 8**. Vaginal administration of a single-dose of 1200 mg miconazole suppository increased serum concentrations of EE. Specifically, AUCDay8-10 and AUCDay8-21 were increased by approximately 18% and 67%, respectively. However, AUCDay8-9, results representing the extent of absorption on the day of miconazole suppository administration, were similar between the control cycle and
L1517: the interaction cycle. Following a single-dose of vaginal administration of 1200 mg miconazole suppository, SA AUCDay8-9, AUCDay8-10 and AUCDay8-21 were increased by approximately 30%, 32% and 19%, respectively.

L1532: **Safety Results:** Similar profiles were found in the frequency and intensity of AEs for the CVS only and the miconazole co-medication treatment groups.
L1533: ## **Conclusions:**
L1534: - Systemic exposure of SA and EE increased when a single or multiple doses of miconazole nitrate suppository were co-administered with the CVS, with up to a maximum EE geometric mean ratio (GMR) AUCDay8-21 of 1.67 (1.51 - 1.86) for the single dose suppository and 1.42 (1.21-1.66) for the multiple dose suppository.
- By contrast, systemic exposure of SA and EE was bioequivalent with and without miconazole nitrate cream (all GMRs and confidence intervals within 0.80 to 1.25).
L1535: ## **Reviewer's Comments:**
L1536: - *Mechanism of Interaction*
L1537: *The data suggest that lipophilic oil-based miconazole vaginal suppositories enhanced the release of lipophilic steroids such as SA and EE from the CVS, whereas a water-based vaginal cream did not. These findings suggest that the active ingredient, miconazole does not affect the release of SA and EE from the CVS; but the inactive ingredients in the suppository formulation have an effect on the release of SA and EE from the CVS.*
L1538: - **Long-term Effect on CVS's performance**
*The effect on EE release seems to last beyond the duration of co-administration. For example, when a single dose of vaginal suppository was co-administered with CVS on Day 8, there was only 18% increase in AUC_{Day8-10} compared to the control cycle, whereas there was a 67% increase in AUC_{Day8-21}. This may indicate a change in the release mechanism of EE from CVS and it is not known whether the effect will persist through the following cycles.*
- **Labeling Recommendation**
*Considering the potential long-term effect on the ring's performance, the review team recommends that concurrent use of oil-based vaginal products should not occur with SA/EE CVS use. If there is a need to treat a vaginal condition, water-based vaginal cream or oral therapy may be used concurrently with the ring.*
L1539: ## 3.2. Study XPD-17-301 (PBPK modeling)
L1540: **Title:** Physiologically-based PK (PBPK) modeling of the impact of CYP3A inhibition and induction on systemic exposure to parenterally administered segesterone (alone or with EE)
L1541: **Objective:** To predict the impact of CYP3A inhibitors and inducers on systemic exposure to parenterally administered nesterone (b) (4)
L1542: **Background:** SA is a progestin under development as a potential contraceptive agent, either alone or in combination with EE. The intended route of administration is parenteral, including (b) (4), contraceptive vaginal ring, (b) (4). Mass balance study in rat suggested that metabolism is the primary determinant of the systemic elimination of parenterally administered nesterone. Physicochemical properties of SA (e.g. low molecular weight [370 g/mol], high logP [3.64], low topological polar surface area [60 Å2], a lack of ionizable groups in pH 1-8) and structural similarity to many other steroids predicted high passive diffusion and metabolism by reductive enzymes in liver cytosol and by oxidative, reductive and/or hydrolytic enzymes in liver microsomes. The Applicant did not conduct a mass balance study or dedicated drug-drug interaction (DDI) studies in human.
L1543: The Applicant used a custom-built PK model and in-vitro datasets to predict the impact of CYP3A inhibitors and inducers on SA PK. This review evaluates adequacy of the Applicant's approach in the prediction of DDI between SA and CYP3A inhibitor/inducer.
L1544: **Methods:**
L1545: (b) (4)
L1546: ## **Reviewer's Comments:**
L1547: 1. *The applicant's model is not adequate to support* (b) (4)

2. *According to FDA Guidance for Industry - In Vitro Metabolism and Transporter Mediated Drug-Drug Interaction Studies, the contribution of a specific metabolizing enzyme to an investigational drug's clearance is considered significant if the enzyme is responsible for &gt;25% of the drug's elimination based on in vitro phenotyping studies and human PK data.* (b) (4)

*The applicant should conduct clinical DDI studies using strong index inhibitors and/or inducers of the enzyme.*
L1548: ## 4. Clinical PK/PD Assessment
L1549: ## 4.1. Study 124 (Pilot Dose-Finding Study)
L1550: **Title:** ST-1435 (SA) Implant Dose-Finding Study8
L1551: ## **Objectives:**
L1552: - To determine which of the 2 doses of ST-1435 (SA) delivered by the implant regimens was better for contraceptive purposes
- To determine the plasma levels of SA produced by both implants and the association of these levels with ovulation inhibition and with cervical mucus effects
L1553: **Study Design:** This was a parallel group, open-label, multi-sites, dose-finding study in 120 healthy women with regular menstrual periods. Study subjects were randomized to one of the two SA implant treatment groups:
L1554: - 100 µg/day dose (n = 60): Twenty (20) subjects at each of the 3 sites received one 4 cm SA implant (containing 80 mg of SA)
- 150 µg/day dose (n = 60): Twenty (20) subjects at each of the 3 sites received two 3 cm implants (containing a total of 120 mg of SA).
L1555: The implants were inserted by the fifth day of the menstrual period which occurred after the pretreatment cycle. Subjects participated in the study for 2 years.
L1556: Blood samples for P and E2 were taken twice a week for 6 weeks consecutively beginning at pre-specified time intervals (at months 1, 6, 12, 18, and 24 post-insertion). Vaginal ultrasound measurements were performed twice a week for 6 weeks in a subset of women (N=23/dose) to examine the effect of SA on endometrial thickness and ovarian follicles. Luteal activity was defined as at least 1 progesterone measurement above 10 nmol/L and/or ovulation documented by ultrasound. Other PD parameters such as Cervical Mucus, Endometrial Biopsy and Menstrual Diary Cards for Bleeding Patterns were also investigated in the study. SA serum concentrations were measured once a week in the same sampling periods as for P and E2.
L1557: Serum concentrations of SA were measured using a validated radioimmunoassay at the (b) (4) University of Helsinki in Finland. The assay used [125I] iodohistamine-CMO- NES as tracer. The antiserum was raised against NES-CMO- BSA in rabbits. The method gave linear results within the concentration range of 13-432 pmol/L. Recovery was between 100%-110%. The intra- and inter-assay CV were 5%-9% and 10%-17%, respectively.
L1558: **PD Results:** Table 14 shows the results of luteal activity based on a combination of P levels and vaginal ultrasound. No luteal activity was observed through the first 6 months of the study in either treatment group, and very little activity was observed at Month 12. However, at 18 months, the frequency of luteal activity in the low-dose group rose to 26.7% of cycles, as
L1559: 8 Brache, V.; Massai, R.; Mishell, D. R.; Moo-Young, A. J.; Alvarez, F.; Salvatierra, A. M.; Cochon, L.; Croxatto, H.; Robbins, A.; Faundes, A., Ovarian function during use of Nestorone(R) subdermal implants. Contraception 2000, 61 (3), 199-204
L1560: compared with only 2.4% of cycles in the high-dose group. A further increase to 34.9% vs 16.2%, respectively, was observed at 24 months.
L1561: **Table 14. Frequency of Cycles with Luteal Activity (P &gt; 10 \text{ nmol/L}) and/or Ovulation by Ultrasound**

L1563: NES = SA. a Three subjects in the SA 100 \mu\text{g/day} and 5 subjects in the 150 \mu\text{g/day} treatment groups had not yet completed Year 2 of the study at the time of this analysis; therefore, Year 2 data for these 8 subjects were not included in the analysis.
L1564: Estrogen suppression, as shown by E2 concentrations constantly \le 100 \text{ pmol/L}, was only observed during the first 6 months of use. The frequency with which subjects had E2 levels in the high range (&gt; 1500 \text{ pmol/L}) increased from less than 10% in Month 1 to approximately 24% in the high-dose group and 35% in the low-dose group in Month 24. There were reduced mean cervical mucus maximum scores and decreased sperm penetration for subjects in both treatment groups at Months 18 and 24 compared to the Pretreatment Cycle.
L1565: Breakthrough bleeding and spotting episodes of 3 to 5 per 3-month period occurred in 50% or more of subjects in both treatment groups, and prolonged or irregular bleeding occurred in 20% or more of subjects in most 3-month periods in both treatment groups. Endometrial biopsy results showed no evidence of pathology in either treatment group.
L1566: **PK Results:** Serum levels of SA declined continuously with time, with a more pronounced drop during the first year of use (**Figure 13**). Mean serum concentrations of SA observed during the first month of use were near 110 and 160 \text{pmol/L}, declining to 60 and 85 \text{pmol/L} at 12 months for 100 \mu\text{g/day} and 150 \mu\text{g/day} SA treatment groups, respectively. The mean serum concentrations stabilized between 18 and 24 months of use at 50 and 70 \text{pmol/L}, for the low- and high-dose groups, respectively. The levels achieved with the lower dose were approximately two-thirds the levels provided by the higher dose.
L1567: When the mean serum SA concentrations in the sampling period were correlated with the frequency of luteal activity, a dose-relationship was observed. No luteal activity was observed in 244 sampling periods with serum SA levels &gt; 80 \text{ pmol/L}. As serum SA levels decreased, the frequency of luteal activity increased.
L1568: Figure 13. SA (Nesterone) serum concentrations among users of 100 µg/day (One 4 cm) and 150 µg/day (Two 3 cm) SA Implants
L1569: **Safety Results:** One subject in the 150 µg/day treatment group discontinued from the study and received a final diagnosis of encephalitis 3 months later from her neurologist. This event was not reported by the investigator as an SAE; however, it is considered to be an SAE by the applicant. No laboratory findings in the study were considered to be clinically significant for either treatment group.
L1570: **Pregnancy:** A single pregnancy occurred for a subject in the 100 µg/day treatment group at 24 months.
L1571: ## **Conclusions:**
L1572: - Serum concentrations of SA declined with time, with a more pronounced decrease during the first 18 months of implant use; thereafter, SA levels remained stable until the end of the study at 24 months.
- Luteal activity was very infrequent during the first year of use (&lt;3%) but increased during the second year, occurring in 27% and 35% of the sampling periods in the 100 µg/day SA treatment group, and 2% and 16% of the sampling periods in the 150 µg/day SA treatment group, at months 18 and 24 of use, respectively.
- No luteal activity was observed with SA levels above 80 pmol/L.
- The SA-only implants in both treatment groups were well tolerated, based on the discontinuation data and clinical laboratory evaluations.
L1573: ## **Reviewer's Comments:**
L1574: *The study showed the exposure-response of SA on ovulation suppression. Per the applicant, the lowest effective SA concentration is around 80 pmol/L, as no luteal activity was observed with SA levels above this level.*
L1575: ## **Reviewer's Notes:**
L1576: *The reported SA doses of two implants are different between the study synopsis and the published article. The synopsis stated SA doses of 150 and 100 µg/day, whereas the publication reported doses of 75 and 50 µg/day. Based on SA serum concentrations, the study was most likely conducted using 75 and 50 µg/day SA doses.*
L1577: ## 4.2. Study 174 (Pilot Dose-finding Study)9
L1578: **Title:** Phase 2 Study of SA Rings
L1579: **Objective:** To estimate the contraceptive efficacy and bleeding pattern of 3 doses of SA CVS.
L1580: **Study Design:** This was a parallel group, open-label, multi-site study in 180 healthy women at risk for pregnancy and with regular menstrual periods. It was a 6-month (7-cycle) continuous use dose-finding study in women using one of 3 different doses (50, 75, or 100 µg/day) of SA CVS as their only method of contraception.
L1581: Blood samples for progesterone and estradiol were taken twice a week for 5 weeks consecutively during a control cycle and during the 1st, 3rd, and 6th month of use. Sampling periods were classified as having luteal activity if P levels were \ge 10 nmol/L in at least one sample. SA serum concentrations were also measured once a week in the same sampling periods as PD measurements.
L1582: Serum concentrations of SA were measured using a validated radioimmunoassay at the Steroid Research Laboratory of the University of Helsinki in Finland, according to the method of Lahteenmaki.
L1583: **PD Results:** Luteal activity was observed in 1.2 to 2.6% of the cycles studied with no detectable difference by dose. The lack of dose-response is likely due to the relatively high SA serum levels were achieved in all 3 SA doses (**Table 15**). Among 5 cycles with luteal activity, two cycles (one in the low dose group and one in the high dose group) had P levels &lt;20 nmol/L (insufficient luteal function) and 3 cycles showed highest P values ranging from 23.5 to 29.6 nmol/L. The E2 levels during the sampling periods were inversely correlated with the dose of SA (**Figure 14**). Estradiol levels remained relatively constant in women using the 75 µg/day and 100 µg/day CVSs during the three sampling periods. Estradiol levels among users of the 50 µg/day CVS were significantly higher than with the other 2 treatment groups (p &lt; 0.01) and had a greater variability among subjects. Particularly, E2 mean concentrations ranged from 716 pmol/L at month 1 to 1,038 pmol/L at month 3 and 1,276 pmol/L at month 6, indicating greater follicular activity with anovulation. In contrast, the highest mean E2 level in the control cycle was between 800–900 pmol/L. The incidence, by cycle, of unscheduled bleeding or spotting over 6 continuous cycles of use, ranged from 63% to 76% in 50 µg/day CVS dose group, 53% to 63% in 75 µg CVS dose group, and 45% to 57% in 100 µg CVS dose group.

L1588: **PK Results:** Mean serum SA concentrations were highest in Cycle 1 and decreased from Cycle 1 to Cycle 7. SA serum concentrations were dose related. Mean serum concentrations of SA from 50, 75 and 100 \mu\text{g/day} CVS were all above 100 \text{pmol/L} at Cycle 7 (**Figure 15**)
L1589: **Figure 15.** Mean SA Serum Concentrations during 6 Months Use of 3 Different CVS Delivering 50, 75, and 100 \mu\text{g/day} of SA
L1590: **Safety Results:** Of the 179 subjects in the safety population, 152 subjects (85%) experienced at least 1 AE. All 3 dose groups were comparable with respect to overall AE profiles and AEs leading to premature study discontinuation. There was no safety signal observed in any of the dose groups with regard to clinical chemistry, hematology, or gynecologic examinations.

**Pregnancy:** Two subjects reported a pregnancy during CVS use: one in 75 \mug/day CVS dose group and one in 100 \mug/day CVS dose group. Neither subjects had serum P measurements.

L1593: - (b) (4) the product in the current submission is a fixed-dose combination of SA and EE administered under a 21/7-day in/out regimen.
- Different bioanalytical techniques (i.e., RIA vs. GC-MS/MS) were used to determine SA serum concentrations and the values from one assay may not be interchangeable with another assay.

L1597: ## 4.3. Study 180 (Pivotal Dose-finding Study)10
L1598: **Title:** *Study of SA® Progestin/Ethinyl Estradiol Contraceptive Rings: Comparison of 200/15, 150/20, and 150/15 Doses*
L1599: **Objectives:** To determine if an increase in SA or EE dose would significantly improve ring performance compared to the SA/EE 150/15 CVS
L1600: **Study Design:** This was a 13-cycle, multicenter, randomized, parallel-group, dose-ranging study to compare the performance of SA/EE CVS in 3 different dose combinations. One hundred fifty (150) pre-menopausal women were randomized to one of the following treatment groups: SA/EE 150/15 CVS (N=50), SA/EE 150/20 CVS (N=50), or SA/EE 200/15 CVS (N=50). The CVS was to be used continuously for 21 days, removed for 7 days, and then inserted into the vagina to begin the next cycle. This pattern of use was to be replicated for 13 cycles. Subjects were instructed not to remove the CVS during the 21 days of ring use, but were permitted to remove the CVS for periods of no more than 2-hour intervals to allow for intercourse or washing of the CVS.
L1601: P levels were evaluated off-therapy at Pre-treatment and Post-treatment, and were also evaluated on-therapy during Weeks 1, 2, and 3 of Cycles 3, 6, 9, and 13. Luteal activity was deemed to have occurred in any cycle in which there was a progesterone measurement \ge 10 nmol/L. At weeks 1, 2, and 3 of Cycles 3, 6, 9 and 13, a single weekly blood draw was scheduled to measure SA and EE concentrations.
L1602: ## Bioanalytical Method
L1603: Serum concentrations of SA were measured using a validated radioimmunoassay developed by (b) (4) Family Federation of Finland Helsinki, Finland. The validated quantitation range was 5.3 to 1350 pmol/L for SA. Serum concentrations of EE were measured by (b) (4) USC Medical Center, Department of OB/GYN (b) (4)
L1604: **PD Results:** The SA/EE 150/15 CVS treatment group had the greatest percent of cycles with luteal activity (10.7%) compared with 150/20 CVS treatment group (2.2%) and 200/15 CVS treatment group (4.3%). Increasing the dose of SA from 150 to 200 \mug further suppressed the luteal activity, but the difference was not statistically significant (**Table 16** and **Table 17**). Increasing the dose of EE from 15 to 20 \mug resulted in a significant decrease in luteal activity and a better bleeding control. However, subjects in this study group also reported more AEs related to the administration of EE (see safety results).

L1610: **PK Results:** The higher dose SA and EE CVSs were associated with higher plasma levels of SA and EE through 13 cycles. Particularly, SA/EE 200/15 CVS group showed the highest SA concentrations compared to the other dose groups. Interestingly, SA concentrations from SA/EE 150/15 CVS group were lower at most of timepoints than those from SA/EE 150/20 CVS group (Figure 17). SA/EE 150/20 CVS group had higher EE concentrations than the 150/15 and 200/15 CVS groups which each had similar EE concentrations (Figure 18).
L1611: Figure 17. Mean SA Serum Concentrations from SA/EE 150/15 CVS (Treatment A), SA/EE 150/20 CVS (Treatment B), SA/EE 200/15 CVS (Treatment C) for Weeks 1, 2, and 3 in Cycles 3, 6, 9, and 13

L1613: **Safety Results:** All 3 treatment groups were comparable with respect to AEs leading to premature study discontinuation; however, subjects in the SA/EE 150/20 CVS treatment group reported more nausea and vulvovaginal pruritus, and experienced 2 SAEs related to the administration of EE (cholecystitis and superficial thrombophlebitis). Colposcopic findings were comparable across treatment groups.
L1614: **Pregnancy:** During the study, no pregnancies occurred in users of the 150/15 and 150/20 rings, but there were two unintended pregnancies in the 200/15 dose group.
L1615: **Conclusions:** SA/EE 150/15 CVS treatment group had acceptable ovulation inhibition, an acceptable bleeding profile, and an acceptable AE profile compared to the higher dose treatment groups. Given the relatively lower systemic exposure of SA and EE compared to the other two studied dose groups, the SA/EE 150/15 dose was selected for further studies.
L1616: ## **Reviewer's Comments**
L1617: *With the same SA and EE dose, the current study showed 10% of cycles with luteal activity as compared to 2% in Study 300PK. The reason for this discrepancy is not known.*
L1618: *This reviewer plotted the Progesterone vs. SA concentrations using data from the current study (Figure 19 and Figure 20).*
L1619: *No clear exposure-response was observed from this study.*
L1620: Figure 19 Observed P vs SA concentrations in Study 180
L1621: Figure 20 SA Concentrations in Subjects with Luteal Activity
L1622: During the late-cycle meeting with the applicant on May 30, 2018, the review team informed the applicant that data from Study 180 (b) (4) and requested a detailed analysis for this study. The applicant responded to this information request on June 12, 2018. The applicant stated that (b) (4) for study 180 as had been noted in study 174 (NDA Amendment 0020). Plots of the data
L1623: by cycle and by dose also did not show a threshold effect for ovulation inhibition. When the data were plotted by site, it became apparent that all of the ovulations (progesterone levels &gt; 10 nmol/L) in this study occurred at one site, the Los Angeles, California site. There were no ovulations at the sites in: Helsinki, Finland; Santiago, Chile; Santo Domingo, Dominican Republic; or St. Louis, Missouri......Given the consistent ovulation inhibition for 5 of the 6 sites in study 180, and the evidence for ovulation inhibition with a dose effect in study 174, it is possible (and consistent with the findings) that the subjects at the Los Angeles site in study 180 did not adhere to the 21/7 regimen for CVR use but rather used the CVR for less than 21 days in some cycles. In 5 of the 6 sites in study 180, ovulation inhibition was observed that is consistent with the 21/7 use of a combined hormonal contraceptive, the result from the sixth site is an outlier."
L1624: The review team acknowledges the importance of patient compliance in the efficacy of SA/EE CVS. However, adequate serum concentrations of SA (i.e., median SA concentration &gt; 200 pmol/L) in women with luteal activity at the Los Angeles site in Study 180 does not seem to support the poor patient compliance as suggested by the applicant.
L1625: ## 4.4. Study 181 (Dosing Regimen-finding Study)
L1626: **Title:** *Study of Ovulation Inhibition in Women Using SA Progestin/EE Contraceptive Rings: Comparison of Seven Days and Four Days Out*
L1627: **Objective:** To determine the effectiveness of SA 150 \mu\text{g}/\text{day} / EE 15 \mu\text{g}/\text{day} contraceptive vaginal ring (CVS) in preventing ovulation in subjects following either of 2 schedules: CVS 21 days in/7 days out or CVS 26 days in/4 days out
L1628: **Study Design:** This was a 6-cycle, randomized, multicenter, parallel-group, open-label study of the SA 150 \mu\text{g}/\text{day} / EE 15 \mu\text{g}/\text{day} CVS in 101 sexually-active women with regular menstrual periods. The eligible subjects were randomized to one of the following schedules:
L1629: - Schedule A - CVS 21 days in/7 days out (21/7). The CVS was inserted on Day 4 to 6 of Cycle 1, left in continuously for 21 days, and then removed for 7 days. This pattern of 3 weeks of use and 1 week of non-use was followed for 6 cycles using the same CVS.
- Schedule B - CVS 26 days in/4 days out (26/4). The CVS was inserted on Day 4 or 5 of Cycle 1, used for 26 days, and then removed for 4 days. This pattern of 26 days of use followed by 4 days of non-use was followed for 6 cycles using the same CVS.
L1630: Progesterone and estradiol levels were evaluated off-therapy at Pre-treatment and Post-treatment, Cycle, and twice per week during Cycles 2, 4, and 6 of CVS use (8 collections per cycle). As some follicular development has been observed in the hormone-free interval, a blood sample was also collected on the day the CVS was removed in Cycles 1, 3, and 5. Samples were also collected twice per week in the Post-treatment Cycle. Serum levels of SA were determined from blood samples collected once per week (at the end of the week) during Cycles 2, 4, and 6 of CVS use.
L1631: **PD Results:** The mean values for progesterone during each month of the study were suppressed in relation to baseline for subjects in both treatment regimens, with the highest on-therapy maximums occurring in Month 1 (28.5 nmol/L) and Month 3 (37.2 nmol/L) for subjects in the 26/4-day regimen and in Month 6 (28.5 nmol/L) for subjects in the 21/7-day regimen (**Table 18**). The mean values for estradiol during each month of the study were suppressed in relation to baseline for subjects in both treatment regimens, but were higher for the subjects in the 21/7-day regimen compared with the 26/4-day regimen. The absence of unscheduled bleeding and/or spotting occurred at a similar frequency for subjects in the 2 treatment regimens: 56.3% of the subjects in the 21/7-day regimen and 50.0% of the subjects in the 26/4-day regimen had no unscheduled bleeding and/or spotting.
L1632: **Table 18. Concentrations of P and E2 by Month for Subjects Receiving the 21/7- or 26/4-Day Treatment Regimen**
L1633: N=number of subjects; SD=standard deviation.
L1634: Visit windows: Baseline: Days \le 1; Month 1: Days 2-30; Month 2: Days 31-60; Month 3: Days 61-90; Month 4: Days 91-120; Month 5: Days 121-150; Month 6: Days 151-180.
L1635: **PK Results:** SA was assessed for the greatest number of subjects (64) during Month 2. At Month 2, the concentration of SA in the serum was approximately equal for subjects in the 2 regimens (**Table 19**).
L1636: **Table 19. Concentrations of SA by Month for Subjects Receiving the 21/7 or 26/4 Treatment Regimen**
L1637: **Safety Results:** The safety profile of SA /EE 150/15 CVS was similar when used as either a 21/7-day in/out or a 26/4-day in/out regimen. Of the 101 subjects in the Safety population, 80 subjects (79.2%) experienced at least 1 AE. No deaths and 1 serious adverse event were reported during the study.
L1638: **Conclusion:** The 21/7-day regimen of SA /EE 150/15 µg dose was selected for further studies due to the lower SA dose compared to 24/4-day regimen.
L1639: ## 5. Mechanistic Safety Assessment
